. We note that in the first case, the coboundary expansion of
Y_{v}
is constant because all faces are connected to
v
, so the minimal coboundary expansion of the subgraphs really depends on
\min_{v\in{X[i^{\prime}]}}h^{1}(X_{v}^{I^{\prime}})
; this is the reason for the appearence of this expression in the lemma.
We will see below that the agreement complex
\mathcal{A}
is actually (a blow-up of)
X^{I^{\prime}}
.

Let us define everything more formally.

Definition B.1

(
GK(X,I,I^{\prime})
decomposition)
.

Let
GK(X,I,I^{\prime})=(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{A},\nu,\pi)
be as follows.

1.

\mathcal{Y}=\left\{Y_{v}\left|\;\vphantom{Y_{v}v\in X^{I^{\prime}}(0)}\right.v\in X^{I^{\prime}}(0)\right\}
are the following.

(a)

When
col(v)=i^{\prime}
then
Y_{v}=X_{v}^{I}
.

(b)

When
col(v)\neq i^{\prime}
then
Y_{v}
is the complex induced by
\{v\}\cup X_{v}^{I\setminus\{col(v)\}}(0)
. We note that in this case
Y_{v}\cong(X_{v}^{I\setminus\{col(v)\}})^{*}
, because every face in
X_{v}^{I\setminus\{col(v)\}}
is connected to
v
.

2.

Let
\mathcal{A}(0)=X^{I^{\prime}}(0)
(technically
\mathcal{A}(0)=\{Y_{v}\}
but it is shorter to identify
Y_{v}\in\mathcal{A}(0)
with
v
). An edge
\{v,v^{\prime}\}_{v^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathcal{A}(1)
if and only if
v\neq v^{\prime}
and
\{v,v^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}\}\in X

(either they form a triangle or it could be that
v^{\prime\prime}=v
or
v^{\prime\prime}=v^{\prime}
).
Similarly a triangle
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}_{v_{4},v_{5},v_{6}}\in\mathcal{A}(2)
if and only if
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\},\{v_{4},v_{5},v_{6}\}\in X(2)

and
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},v_{5},v_{6}\}\in X

(note that some of these spaces may be equal).

3.

We define
\pi
as follows. We choose
\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}_{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}}
by:

(a)

We sample
w=\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},v_{5}\}\in X^{I\cup\{i^{\prime}\}}(4)
.

(b)

We sample
w^{\prime}=\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}\subseteq w
uniformly given that
col(w^{\prime})\subseteq I^{\prime}
. We randomly reorder
w^{\prime}
.

(c)

We sample
w^{\prime\prime}=\{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}\}\subseteq w
uniformly given that
col(w^{\prime\prime})\subseteq I
, independent from the previous step. We randomly reorder
w^{\prime\prime}
.

(d)

We randomly output
\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}_{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}}
.

4.

We define
\nu
to be a marginal of
\pi
. In particular, after sampling
\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}_{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}}\sim\pi
we choose
v\in\{u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}\}
uniformly at random and output
(Y_{v},\{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}\})
.

The following proposition describes the main method by which we apply this decomposition.

Proof of
Lemma
8.10
.

We use the decomposition
GK(X,I,I^{\prime})
in order to apply
Theorem
5.4
. Let us calculate the parameters.

–

Coboundary expansion of every
Y_{v}
.
Fix
v
. If
col(v)\in I
then
Y_{v}
is a partite complex such that one part has only one vertex, i.e.
v
itself, since
v
is the only subspace compatible with itself of dimension
col(v)
. It follows from
Claim
2.8.1
that
h^{1}(Y_{v})=\Omega(1)
. The other case is when
col(v)=i^{\prime}_{j}
, in which case its coboundary expansion some
h^{1}(X_{v}^{I})
since
Y_{v}\cong X_{v}^{I}
. Thus the expansion of every sub complex is
\min\{\min_{v\in{X[i^{\prime}]}}h^{1}(X_{v}^{I}),\Omega(1)\}=\Omega(\min_{v\in{X[i^{\prime}]}}h^{1}(X_{v}^{I}))
.

–

The expansion of
\mathcal{A}
.
It is easy to see that
\mathcal{A}
is a blow-up of
X^{I^{\prime}}
because ignoring the labels,
\pi
just samples a triangle from
X^{I^{\prime}}(2)
uniformly at random. We define
\gamma=h^{1}(X^{I^{\prime}})
. If we show that for every (unlabeled) edge
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}\in X^{I^{\prime}}(1)
, the label graph is a constant edge expander, it will follow from
Lemma
6.3
that
h^{1}(\mathcal{A})=\Omega(\gamma)
. Indeed fix any
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
of colors (say)
\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
. The labels of this edge are all
u
such that there is a flag that contains both
u
and
v_{1},v_{2}
(including the case that
u
equals one of
v_{1},v_{2}
).

Consider the following
5
-partite complex
Z
that corresponds to the distribution
\pi
conditioned on
v_{1},v_{2}
. The parts are

Z[1]=\{v_{1}\},\;Z[2]=\{v_{2}\},\;Z[3]=X_{v_{1},v_{2}}[i_{3}],\;Z[4]=X_{v_{1},v_{2}}[i_{4}],\;Z[5]=X_{v_{1},v_{2}}[i_{5}]

The top level faces are all
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},v_{5}\}\in X^{I\cup I^{\prime}}(4)
(with the same distribution as of the first step in the description of
\pi
). Note that every labeled triangle
\{v_{1},v_{2},u_{3}\}_{u_{4},u_{5},u_{6}}
that contains
v_{1},v_{2}
corresponds to a flag
\{v_{1},v_{2},u_{3},u_{4},u_{5}\}\in X^{I\cup I^{\prime}}(4)
. This complex
Z
is a local spectral expander because
X_{v_{1},v_{2}}^{i_{3},i_{4},i_{5}}
is a local spectral expander: fix a link
Z_{t}
. The walk between every two sides in
Z_{t}
is either a complete bipartite graph (if at least one of the sides is
Z_{t}[1]
or
Z_{t}[2]
) or corresponds to a walk in two sides of
X_{t\cup\{v_{1},v_{2}\}}
.

Given
u
, we traverse to another
u^{\prime}
by first selecting a triangle
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}_{u,u_{2},u_{3}}
(where
v_{1},v_{2}
and
u
are fixed). Then we select a triangle
\{v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}\}_{u^{\prime},u_{2},u_{3}}
(where all but
u^{\prime}
are fixed). This is the same as doing two steps in the vertex vs triangle swap walk in
Z
, i.e.
S_{0,2}(Z)
. By
Claim
2.4.1
this is a constant spectral (and hence edge) expander.

We conclude that
h^{1}(\mathcal{A})=\Omega(\gamma)
.

–

The expansion of the local graphs
\mathcal{A}^{u}
.
First we note that in this case the set of elements that appear in the intersection of at least two
Y_{v}
’s is all
X^{I}(0)
. For every
u\in X^{I}(0)
the vertices in
\mathcal{A}^{u}
are all
X_{u}^{I^{\prime}}(0)
, with edges sampled according to the distribution of
X_{u}^{I^{\prime}}(1)
. By assumption this graph has
\zeta=\Theta(1)
edge expansion.

–

Smoothness of distributions.
We note that
\pi_{2}=\nu_{2}=\mu_{2,S^{I}}
so all three are
1
-smooth. Moreover, the distributions
\nu_{1,y}=\pi_{1,y}
and
\nu_{0,y}=\pi_{0,y}
so they are also
1
-smooth. All five items of smoothness follow.

Hence we apply
Theorem
5.4
to
GK(X,I,I^{\prime})
with parameters
\alpha=1,\Omega(\beta),\Omega(\gamma),\zeta=\Omega(1)
which shows that
h^{1}(X^{I})\geqslant\Omega(\beta\gamma)
as required.
∎

We note that the actual requirements from this theorem are that the actual requirements of expansion in this theorem are that the swap walks in the links have some constant edge expansion (even
\frac{1}{3}-\varepsilon
for any constant
\varepsilon>0
), but the constant behind the
\Omega
notation tends to
0
as the edge expansion tends to
0
. This version suites our purposes.
